FILE NOT E

REPORT OF AN UNEXPLAINED AERIAL SIGHTING

1. Fusection 40 | rang (work telephone no
Section 40) Last night she saw white bright flashing moving
ights in the sky over Burnside, near GLASGOW. The time was

2340 hres. .

2. I explained our role and that we knew of no evidence to
support extraterrestrial lifeforms so the most likely
explanation was that it could have been several aircraft lights
which were nowhere near each other but by a quirk of
perspective appeared all in close proximity with one another.

3. I said if there were any sightings in the vicinity
reported to us in the coming week we would contact her to let
her know. She seem quite happy with this. She was not a
“believer”, but was just generally interested in case we might

know what it was.

RAF POLICE SITUATION REPORT FOR THE PERIOD
0800 HRS TUE 14 MAY 96 TO 0759 HRS WED 15 96

1. For your information, during the reporting period, a number
of incidents occurred which were reported to and actioned by the
RAF Police, which involved RAF St Mawgan, its property, personnel
and environment.

2. Brief details of the incidents are outlined below:
: RAF POLICE

DOB No TIME

734/1/96 00i5hrs

DETAILS OF OCCURRENCE

UFO SIGHTING REPORT

ATT this time the duty PBX operator,SAC Section 40) Fe seeee! this
location, stating that he had a civilian on an exchange line
wishing to report a suspicious object that he believed to be a
UFO hoveri i at Tintagel.The person identified
himself as of Tintagel.He described the object

as being of "enormous size",very bright,triangular in shape with
&9 lights down each of it's sides.

ACTION TAKEN ;

SAC BEGIN was instructed to te11 SEO chat as the object

was so far away from RAF St Mawgan we were unable to assist him
and that if the object was a cause for concern, the only advice
that we could offer him was to inform the local police.

ALSO TO BE NOTED;

At approx 0110hrs the civilian police at Camborne informed us
that they had spoken to SEIU -<caraing the sighting and
that he had further reported the sighting to both the National
press and the Civil Aviation Authority.Due to possible
involveméfit with the press,Fit was informed.

3. _ Should you require further information concerning the above
incidents, I should be happy to brief you at your convenience.

Fit Lt
OC RAF Police

noticed the object with " naked eye, then through binoculars.

heading $ before disappearing. All movements were approx at speed pimilar to that of a

shooting star. !

I was accompanied by:
Rank: Constable Number: [iin 40 -Div/Dept: —- Westem/Perth
when the statement was noted.

The statement was not signed in my notebook by the witness.

STATES:-
‘About 2040 hours, Tuesday Sind April 1996, I was driving northwards on the Al, about 30 miles south of
Edinburgh (near large cement works), when I saw two Orange/Red coloured circular lights in the sky one on

top of the other. They appeared to be flying at the height of an airliner, they shot across the sky in a westerly
direction but I would say their speed was at least eight times faster than a normal jet.

I pulled into a layby to watch these objects, after I had parked up ] noticed one of the objects had disappeared.
The other appeared to be hovering, I couldn’t understand how it had come to stop so quickly due to the speed
it travelled across the sky. The object then began to move slowly southwards and the whole thing was
flashing in and out. I watched it until it disappeared out of sight.

The sky was clear and the weather was fine.

10. Nearby Objects (Telephone or Fower Lines, Lakes, Rivers, High
Structures, Airfields, Generating Flants, Factories, Pits er Other Sites with

Floodlights or Night-lights):

1, Members of the public who submit reports should be informed that their
reports have been passed to the Ministry of Defence.

2. Sightings by Service personnel and action taken as a result of civiliai:.
sightings must not ke disclosed to members of the Press who, if they make
enquiries, should be referred to the Ministry of Defence.

3. A routine unclassified signal, or Fax (Ext SER shou1d be sent to
Ministzy of Defence, Londen (for the attention of MOD Sec(AS)2A). The format
of the signal is ag set out in the repert above. ‘The signal SIC is 26F.

4. Contact MOD Sec(AS)2a as soon as possible at MOD Main Building Ext BERs 40

FILE NOTE

REPORT OF AN UNEXPLAINED AERIAL SIGHTING

1. Received a call from Section40. Last
Saturday morning (22 Feb), at 0215 hours, she and a friend were
driving home north of Peebles and they saw two or three
distinctive very bright lights in a field, hovering.

2. I explained the MOD position on "UFOs" and offered to let
her know if any explanation came to light for the sighting.

She clearly understood that we were not necessarily going to be
able to come up with an explanation, but did not think what she
saw was “extraterrestrial". She was just interest#to know if
someone else had seen it and could say what it was.

@ INITIALLY AIR TRAFFIC CONTROL AT WADDINGTON REFORTED THEY COULD NOT
SEE ANYTHING ON PRIMARY OR SECONDARY RADAR. LATER AT ABOUT 1930 AIR
TRAFFIC REPORTED THAT THEY COULD SEE WHAT THEY CONSIDER TO BE
ANONYMOUS PROPAGATION VISIBLE ON THE RADAR IN THE AREA THAT

REPORTED HIS SIGHTING. THESE WERE OF AN INTERMITTENT NATURE AND SPEED

AND WOULD NOT INDICATE PRIMARY ACTS. THESE AND FROFG WERE
EVIDENT ELSEWHERE ON THE RADAR. WAS NOT INFORMED OF THIS

Notes on correct completion of UFO report

A. local times to be quoted.

B. Number of objects, size, shape, colours, brightness, sound, smell ete.
C. Geographical location. Indoors or outdoors, Stationary or moving.

BD. Naked eye, binoculars, other optical device, still or movie cameras

&. A landmark may be more useful than a badly estimated bearing...

F. Estimated heights are unreliable. .

G. By reference to a known landmerk wherever possible.

H. Changes in E, F and G may be of more use then estimates of course and
speed.

I. Moving clouds, haze, mist, etc.

J. Telephone lines; high voltage lines, reservoir, lake or dam; swamp or
marshy river, high buildings, tall chimneys, steeples, spires, TV or
radio masts; airfields, generating plant; factories, pits or other
sites with flood-lights or other night lighting.

K. Police, military organisations, the press etc.

Comment :

0709252 FEB 96

1 minute

Was cube-like in shape although the top. was
bigger than the bottom giving it a ‘metal cup
cake' appearance. Had reddish glow to it. Was
the size of ‘several detached houses'

Driving northbound on M3, just past the M25
junction. 'UFO' was about two miles away on the
right. Seen with naked eye.

First assumption was that it was a warning light
on a building or mast so, several hundred feet.

Hovered for 60 secs then flew eastwards at very
high speed.

Slightly misty with generally low cloud cover.

No, only work colleagues.

No-one specific although the M3 was very busy so
‘someone must have seen something’.

Section 40) | seemed quite calm and rational

about the whole thing and understood that we
were only interested from a defence viewpoint.
He did mention that he believed a similar
sighting was made at Manchester Airport the same
day (I didn't tell him that he had misread the
reports) Meecion40 | was particularly
interested in whether anyone else had reported
the UFO.

REPORT OF AN UNEXPLAINED AERIAL SIGHTING

1. Date, time &
duration of sighting

2. Description of object
(No of objects, size, shape,
colour, brightness, noise)

3. Exact position of observer
(Indoors/outdoors,
stationary/moving)

4. How observed (Naked eye,
binoculars, other optical
device, camera or camcorder)

5. Direction in which object
first seen (A landmark may be
more useful than a roughly
estimated bearing) ~

6. Angle of sight (Estimated
heights are unreliable)

7. Distance (By reference to a
known landmark)

8. Movements (Changes in 5, 6 & 7
may be of more use than
estimates of course and speed)

Be

9. Met conditions during observations CL
(Moving clouds, haze, mist etc)

10. Nearby objects (Telephone lines,
high voltage lines, reservoir, lake
or dam, swamp or marsh, river, high
buildings, tall chimneys, steeples,
spires, TV or radio masts,
airfields, generating plant,
factories, pits or other sites with
floodlights or night lighting)

11. To whom reported (Police, military,
press etc)

12. Name, address and telephone
no of informant

13. Any further detail volunteered

ates o

14. Other witnesses

15. Date and time of receipt

16. Remarks

REPORT OF AN UNEXPLAINED AERIAL SIGHTING

1. Date, time &
duration of sighting

Description of object
(No of objects, size, shape,
colour, brightness, noise)

3. Exact position of observer
(Indoors/outdoors,
stationary/moving)

4. How observed (Naked eye,
binoculars, other optical
device, camera or camcorder)

5. Direction in which object
first seen (A landmark may be
more useful than a roughly
estimated bearing)

6. Angle of sight (Estimated
heights are unreliable)

Distance (By reference to a
known landmark)

Movements (Changes in 5, 6 & 7
may be of more use than
estimates of course and speed)

Met conditions during observations
(Moving clouds, haze, mist etc)

10. Nearby objects (Telephone lines,
high voltage lines, reservoir, lake
or dam, swamp or marsh, river, high
buildings, tall chimneys, steeples,
spires, TV or radio masts,
airfields, generating plant,
factories, pits or other sites with
floodlights or night lighting)

11. To whom reported (Police, military,
press etc)
12. Name, address and telephone
no of informant
13. Any further detail volunteered
14. Other witnesses
15. Date and time of receipt lis GANGARY 1S) S0 |

16.

Remarks
